#7275. Security Engineer, Vulnerability Management at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the voice-driven era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice AI applications for OpenAI, xAI, Salesforce, Coursera, Spotify, and thousands of others, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year.

Our customers trust us with audio, video and data belonging to their own end users, so a security failure on our side puts their data and their business at risk. We are hiring a Security Engineer to build and run our vulnerability management and security operations program. Vulnerability management is a core capability for keeping our security posture strong as the platform and the company grow: knowing what we run, finding what is exposed, and getting the right fixes shipped quickly. It also underpins our customer commitments and the compliance programs we are building. Our program is early, and you will shape it. You will join a small Trust team that covers security, IT and compliance, and you will own this program end to end from your first week.

think like both a builder and a breaker, and understand security from first principles

take an automation-first and pragmatic approach to risk mitigation

translate security concerns into engineering action

can clearly explain complex technical concepts to others

are a fast learner and thrive in ambiguity

Select, deploy and operationalize scanning tooling across our production assets.

Run vulnerability management end to end: triage, severity in our environment, owner assignment, remediation SLAs and reporting.

Build engineering-first triage and remediation workflows that fit how our engineering teams already work, and automate the routing and follow-up.

Build a remediation dashboard that engineering and leadership read and act on.

Automate collection of the vulnerability management evidence our compliance programs depend on, in partnership with our Compliance team.

Build automation, including agentic tooling, that removes manual security operations work and keeps pace with engineering growth.

5+ years in security engineering, security operations or software engineering, with a significant share of that focused on security.

Hands-on experience building or running a vulnerability management program or software security tooling (SCA, container, cloud or host scanning).

An engineering-first approach to triage and remediation: you get findings fixed by making the fix easy to ship.

The judgment to set a finding's real severity in our environment, using CVSS as one input.

Strong scripting and automation skills in a language such as Go or Python, and comfort reading infrastructure as code such as Terraform.

A pragmatic approach to risk: you can explain why a finding matters to the business and how fast it needs to be fixed.

Experience with real-time media, networking or WebRTC systems.

Experience supporting compliance audits such as HIPAA, PCI-DSS, ISO 27001 or SOC 2.

Incident response or application security experience.

Experience in AI security, such as securing LLM applications or agent infrastructure.

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

LiveKit is an equal opportunity employer and does not discriminate on the basis of any characteristic protected by applicable law. If you require a reasonable accommodation during the application or interview process, please contact recruiting@livekit.io.

#7274. Want to Sell an Agency? Launch a Creator Marketing Firm

This story was originally published in On Background with Mark Stenberg, a free weekly newsletter that explores the key themes shaping the media industry. You can sign up for it here.

In recent months, the market for creator and social agencies has been, to put it lightly, brisk.

In September, the Madrid-based social agency Samy acquired Get Engaged, a U.S. social and influencer shop, in a deal valued in the low nine figures.

In October, Billion Dollar Boy Group bought Creator Authority, a business-to-business influencer agency, and the private equity firm Shamrock Capital, which owns ADWEEK, made an eight-figure investment in the social-first agency Saylor.

Earlier this month, the social-first agency LV8 merged with its sister company Mirage Digital on the back of a “high eight figures” investment from Velocity Capital Management. And last week, the private equity firm Falfurrias Management Partners took a majority stake in Freeman & Forrest, another B2B creator agency.

The deals are part of a record year, according to James Creech, founder of the creator M&A advisory firm Quartermast. There have been 30 agency acquisitions in the creator economy so far in 2026, up from 20 in all of 2025 and above the previous high of 28 in 2022, according to Quartermast data.

Overall, there have been 124 M&A creator-adjacent deals this year, the busiest on record, and roughly half of the agency deals have come since July, per Quartermast.

Behind the surge is a crop of boutiques founded during the pandemic-era influencer boom that have matured into businesses worth buying, which are run by founders who need more resources to keep up with what clients are asking for.

A specific set of firms—social-first creative shops, podcast agencies, TikTok Shop specialists, and B2B creator plays—are responsible for the majority of this interest, while the deals themselves are increasingly financed by a new cohort of buyers, including private equity firms, strategic roll-ups, and international firms. All of it is unfolding in a frothy market for creator economy deals, which is just now beginning to consolidate in response to urgent demand across a fragmented space.

“Creator marketing works,” Creech said. “Brands and their agency partners are going to continue to invest there.”

The current rush of creator agency activity has its origins in the pandemic, according to LV8 chief executive Griffin Haddrill.

Lockdown acted as a digital accelerant, pulling budgets toward creators at a moment when television and film had dried up and out-of-home advertising had stopped working.

Because acquisitions typically take nine to 12 months to come together, the resulting deal volume spiked in 2021 and 2022, slowed over the following two years, and has since normalized, Creech said.

The same period produced a wave of new agencies, many of which were founded by executives who saw the emerging relevance of the creator ecosystem and launched startups to service it. Five years later, those boutiques have matured into businesses worth buying, according to Haddrill.

FOR Scoops, Sharp Analysis, and Insider Context

For founders, selling offers the resources to keep up with clients, according to Get Engaged co-founder Ben Hiott.

“Social is becoming the center of how brands build their businesses, and clients are asking us to do more every year,” Hiott said in October.

The wave of dealmaking that followed the pandemic was primarily influencer agencies, according to Creech, while this year, the variety of targets has expanded.

In 2026, buyers have gone after social-first creative agencies, such as Get Engaged, OK Cool, and Sway Social; podcast and audio shops like Ad Results Media and Listening Party Agency; and TikTok Shop specialists including Orca, Uptickk, and Yada.

Private equity is also getting more involved in the space, investing capital after years of financing strategic acquirers, as Bridgepoint does with Samy, according to Creech. This year, Shamrock Capital took a majority stake in Saylor, Velocity became a near-equal partner in LV8, and Falfurrias bought Freeman & Forrest, among other deals.

Serial acquirers are consolidating the field as well. Influencer agency Ykone has made four acquisitions in 2026, and creator company network Residence has made two, while Samy and Publicis remain active, according to Creech. After large platform deals for Influential and Captiv8, Publicis has turned to regional acquisitions, including the French creator agency Point d’Orgue, to enter new markets, according to Creech.

For investors, the opportunity lies in stitching these shops together, according to Haddrill.

“The consumer demand was there, but the agency space was fragmented,” Haddrill said. “Now, investors are seeing this fragmentation and working to bring them together.”

B2B creator marketing has also emerged as its own category.

Creator Authority, whose clients include Amazon, Adobe, and Mastercard, was Billion Dollar Boy Group’s first acquisition, an all-cash, multimillion-dollar deal with an earnout component, according to Business Insider.

Freeman & Forrest, founded in 2024 by Emily Freeman and Forrest Brazeal after they ran influencer programs at Microsoft and Google Cloud, helps tech companies like Amazon and Google reach technical customers through creators.

And more of the deals cross borders: Get Engaged gave Samy a U.S. base after the agency built its business in Europe and Latin America, and dealmaking between the U.S. and the U.K. has picked up in both directions, according to Creech.

As the ecosystem matures, the next wave of dealmaking may come from the middle of the market, according to a creator management executive who asked not to be named.

The largest independents, such as Whalar and Influential, have already sold to Accenture Song and Publicis, respectively, which leaves room for midsize independents, many of which will come to market over the next 12 months, the executive said.

Creator management firms, which run creators’ businesses rather than simply booking their brand deals, are likely the next category to consolidate, the executive added.

The buying, in other words, is far from over, according to Creech.

“It’s mature, it’s scaled, it’s professionalized. The work is not done,” Creech said. “We’re maybe entering middle innings.”

Hearst CMO Heads Out (SCOOP): The chief marketing officer at Hearst Magazines, Sherri Chambers, will be leaving the organization in a few weeks, according to sources. Chambers, who joined the digital media firm in December 2024, confirmed the news, while a Hearst Magazines spokesperson expressed their gratitude for Chambers in a statement. The departure is the latest in a series of executive shake-ups. In July, Hearst Magazines appointed Chris Berend as its first chief content and experience officer, and in October, it named A+E executive Juliana Stock as its new president, replacing Debi Chirichella, who will become chair of the group. The legacy media firm has worked hard to cultivate a reputation for calm and consistency in an otherwise turbulent industry, but it certainly looks as if the company is aiming to turn some kind of corner.

Bunny Boss Bolts: The editor in chief of Playboy, Phillip Picardi, has left the organization after less than a year, Puck reported earlier this week. According to a company spokesperson, the move was a personal decision, with Picardi returning to Los Angeles after Playboy set up headquarters in—where else—Miami. But Picardi only began the job officially in March, meaning his tenure at the publisher only lasted a brief eight months. Given all the fanfare surrounding his appointment, I have to imagine something went seriously awry. Or Picardi, who did not return my request for comment, landed an otherwise irrefusable job elsewhere. This is disappointing because I liked the direction Playboy was heading under Picardi, reporting on the state of sex, dating, and relationships in this fraught moment—but maybe the loyal fanbase did not.

BuzzFeed Taps Influential (EXCLUSIVE): On Monday, BuzzFeed named its first chief revenue officer under its new ownership, tapping Chris Poydenis, co-founder of the creator marketing firm Influential, to lead its revenue division. The appointment comes as BuzzFeed attempts an ambitious shift in direction, positioning itself as a source of free, ad-supported television, bundled with the rest of the Allen Media Group portfolio. The decision to name a creator marketing executive as the chief sales lead clearly telegraphs the manner in which BuzzFeed would like to evolve, shifting focus to its creators and video franchise talent. Will it work? The odds are long.

Sara Kehaulani Goo is president of The Washington Post’s creator network, a new division that sits outside of the newsroom and makes sponsored short-form video series with independent creators. Goo, formerly the editor in chief of Axios, launched the first show in April, and the network has since released six series in categories ranging from personal finance to career advice.

Publishers increasingly want to work with creators, but the harder question is why creators would want to work with publishers. In our conversation, Goo and I discussed how The Post’s model works, why its creator content goes through Post fact-checking, and what a media company can offer talent who could go it alone.

Mark Stenberg: You specifically left Axios to head up this new creator division. Can you tell me about the role and how The Post is working with creators?

Sara Kehaulani Goo: I spent 12 years of my career at The Post as a reporter and editor, so it’s been nice to return in a very different role. The theory is simple: Look at where people spend their time, where they get information, where trust is growing, and where revenue is growing across media. The answer to all of those questions is creators. So, we are working with knowledge creators, people who have built big audiences around their expertise, and treating them as equal editorial and business partners.

Sara: We mostly work with creators who’ve built audiences with short-form video in lifestyle topics like personal finance, health and wellness, and career advice. Then, we develop the shows together and publish them together. Last week, we launched Her Next Move with an executive recruiter based in New York. She posts a two-minute video to her audience of a million people as a collab with us, so it reaches both audiences. We amplify it on The Post’s social accounts and publish it on our website and app. That show is sponsored by Uber.

Mark: Why would a creator want to work with the Post? Do they share in the revenue?

Sara: I won’t get into the details, but we pay them for their time. It’s similar to a contributor model, though not exactly like freelancing, because they’re entrepreneurs. A lot of them want to focus on the content, which is what journalists want too. We help build the business around them, and we provide distribution and editorial support when they need it.

Mark: Does their content go through Post fact-checking?

Sara: Yes, through my team. The standards are the same. There’s a lack of consistency in the creator world when it comes to disclosing sponsorships, and to quality in general, and we can bring that. Trust in creators is growing, but people still wonder whether they should trust what they’re watching on Instagram and who paid for it. We can help answer that.

Mark: It’s early, but how big is this now, and where is it headed?

Sara: This year has been a proof of concept. We’ve done six shows with six creators, across health and wellness, personal finance, travel, career advice, and entertainment. Now we’re deciding which ones to renew, and next year we want to do more always-on content. Brands are very interested, because every brand is thinking about its creator strategy, and finding the right creator takes a lot of effort. We take that headache off their shoulders.

Mark: Creators often relish operating on their own. How do you convince them to work with you?

Sara: Having led newsrooms, I’ve always seen my job as spotting talent, developing it, and creating an environment where people can do their best work. This is the same. Creators are entrepreneurs, and the platforms give them plenty of ways to make money on their own. But being an entrepreneur isn’t for everybody, at least not at every level. Media companies have always worked with outside experts. The structure just looks different now.

We’re looking for a Director of Technology Partnerships to lead the teams responsible for growing Supabase through technology integrations and our marketplace ecosystem.

Your job is to turn both into durable sources of revenue, adoption, and customer value.

This role is based in the San Francisco Bay Area, where you’ll build relationships with leading AI, developer tools, and infrastructure companies. Supabase is remote-first, and you’ll work across a globally distributed team.

Lead, coach, and develop the Integrations and Marketplace partnerships teams, including team leads and partner managers.

#7259. Technical Account Manager (APJ) at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re seeking a Technical Account Manager (TAM) to be the primary technical partner for our Team & customers after the sale, helping them adopt, operate, and get long-term value from Supabase.

At Supabase, TAMs are the technical owners of their assigned accounts, working closely with our most valuable customers to:

Understand each customer’s architecture, use cases, constraints, and goals.

Validate deployments and guide customers through onboarding and early success.

Proactively surface risks and optimization opportunities before they become incidents.

Coordinate internal teams on complex escalations and drive root cause analysis.

Align Supabase capabilities with the customer’s roadmap.

Successful TAMs are proactive, technically credible, and customer-first. TAM is a relationship, not a response channel: you’ll act as an extension of your customers’ teams and advocate on their behalf within Supabase.

You’ll be joining our Success team, where you will be expected to lead and innovate around the following areas of responsibility:

Act as the technical owner for your assigned accounts, documenting environments, mapping dependencies, and tracking technical risks.

Validate architecture and deployment plans after the sale, guide initial configuration and best practices, and make sure customers understand platform capabilities and limitations.

Help customers reach early success milestones to shorten time to value and reduce early friction.

Regularly review customer environments and usage patterns to identify scalability, performance, reliability, security, and compliance risks and opportunities.

Plan upgrades and lifecycle events, and run architecture reviews for upcoming initiatives, including capacity and growth planning, feature readiness, and early identification of technical blockers.

Coordinate across Support and Engineering on complex or high-impact issues. TAMs are not frontline support, but provide the context, prioritization, and follow-through that drive resolution, root cause analysis, and preventative action.

Serve as the voice of the customer to Engineering and Product, sharing specific, reproducible feedback on recurring pain points and taking part in beta and early access programs.

Partner with Sales and Customer Success on account health and retention, with technical input on renewals, expansions, and evaluations, and early flags on risks to satisfaction.

6+ years of relevant work experience in engineering, SRE, technical consulting, or similar, including operating production systems under real constraints.

Strong technical depth across database internals, scalability, security posture, and infrastructure tradeoffs. You understand a customer’s architecture as well as they do, without escalating every question.

A proactive mindset: you spot risks and optimization opportunities before the customer does, and you treat your accounts like a personal portfolio.

Sound escalation judgment: you know when to escalate, when to resolve internally, and how to communicate clearly under pressure without creating noise.

Very strong communication skills. TAMs explain complex issues to non-technical executives and root causes to senior engineers, often in the same day.

Awareness of how TAM performance shows up in retention and NRR, including surfacing expansion signals and renewal risks early.

Experience working in async or globally distributed teams.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. Our Sales team needs a dedicated data partner, and creator commerce (how creators monetize by selling digital products) is becoming a bigger part of how Kit and our creators scale. Kit is growing, so we're looking for a Senior Analytics Engineer to own Kit's sales and commerce data from end to end.

Accurate sales data gives our team a clear picture of the pipeline and the confidence to make informed decisions. That’s what makes this role so important.

Your primary focus will be Sales: the dbt models that turn our CRM data into trusted pipeline, deal, and funnel metrics, and the Omni dashboards our sales leader and Senior Revenue Operations Manager use to run the business. You'll also model creator commerce data, so we understand what creators sell and earn through Kit, and you'll pick up reporting work with our Product team, such as feature usage reporting. On a small data team, we step outside our usual lanes when the need is there, so the mix will shift over time.

You'll build for two kinds of consumers: people and AI. Teammates increasingly answer their own questions by querying our semantic layer through Omni's AI and MCP connections to AI tools. That means your models, metric definitions, and documentation need to be clear and correct enough for an AI agent to use them without a data teammate in the loop.

This is a full-time individual contributor role for someone who likes being close to the business, enjoys turning messy operational data into numbers people trust, and is happy to work on both the models and the dashboards that sit on top of them. When Sales and Product have clear, reliable data, we help more creators build more valuable businesses with Kit.

You'll report to Sam Umachi, Head of Data (Linkedin).

Sales and RevOps are your primary partners, who will rely on the models and reporting you own. You'll also partner with our Product team on reporting for features like Visual Automations and on how creators use Kit.

Within our data team, you'll work alongside a Staff Analytics Engineer who owns org-wide semantic layer governance and modeling standards, a Senior Data Engineer who owns ingestion and pipelines, and a Senior Business Analytics Partner focused on revenue opportunities. This role has no direct reports.

First Week: Work through our onboarding curriculum in Notion and meet the data team, our Senior Revenue Operations Manager, the sales team, and your product partners. Get set up in our core tools: dbt Cloud, GitHub, Amazon Redshift, Omni, HubSpot, Linear, Hightouch and Slack. Set up your AI-assisted development environment and learn the shared AI workflows our data team uses. Open and merge your first small pull request so you learn our review and deployment flow early.

First Month: Map how HubSpot objects (deals, pipelines and stages, companies, contacts, and owners) flow into our dbt models, and document the gaps. Take over day-to-day ownership of the sales models and Omni dashboards that teammates have been covering. Audit current sales reporting and list where definitions differ or numbers don't reconcile. Get familiar with how commerce data (products creators sell and the revenue they earn through Kit) lands in the warehouse today. Agree with RevOps and Sales on the most important questions they need answered. Review teammates' pull requests and contribute to our shared SQL, dbt, and documentation standards. Share a written reflection with the team on what you've learned and where you'd focus first.

First Six Months: Ship and maintain well-tested, documented dbt models for core sales metrics, such as pipeline, deal velocity, stage conversion, win rates, and the sales-led funnel. Expose those metrics through the Omni semantic layer, working within the team's governance standards. Rebuild or refresh the core sales dashboards so pipeline reviews and forecasting conversations run on numbers that don't need manual reconciliation. Resolve competing sales metric definitions by writing down the options and tradeoffs, aligning with Sales and RevOps, and documenting the outcome. Model creator-level commerce data, such as products sold, revenue earned through Kit, and conversion, in partnership with data engineering. Write semantic layer descriptions, metric definitions, and documentation that let Omni's AI and MCP-connected AI tools answer routine sales questions correctly, and test them against real questions from Sales, RevOps and Product. Get Sales to the point where routine questions are answered through self-serve, whether in Omni dashboards, Omni's AI, or AI tools connected over MCP, rather than one-off requests.

First Year: Be the recognized owner of Kit's sales data, the person Sales and RevOps go to first and trust without second-guessing. Give our revenue analysis work the sales and commerce models it depends on, so deeper questions about pipeline health and creator earnings can be answered quickly, thereby enabling us to make creators more successful. Leave your domains well documented and well tested, legible to new teammates, stakeholders, and the AI agents that query our semantic layer. Help raise the team's modeling quality through code review, pairing, and sharing what you learn.

Deep dbt experience. You build modular, tested, documented dbt projects, and you're comfortable with incremental models, snapshots, macros, CI, and scheduled jobs in dbt Cloud or similar.

Advanced SQL. You reason carefully about grain, joins, and window functions, and you write queries that are readable and performant on a cloud warehouse.

Data modeling for sales and GTM. You can model deals, pipeline stages and their history, ownership changes, and funnel conversion into clean, reusable structures. You are opinionated on Kimball standards and when a concept should be a fact or dimension.

CRM data fluency. You know how sales data behaves in a CRM, including stage changes, backdated edits, and custom properties. HubSpot is preferred, but other CRM experience (e.g. Salesforce) applies just as well. We care most that you recognize common CRM patterns.

AI-assisted development. You use AI code editors and agents (such as Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, or similar) every day, and you validate their output with tests, query results, and review before anything ships.

Clear written communication. You explain metric definitions, tradeoffs, and data caveats in plain language that Sales, RevOps, and Product partners understand.

BI and semantic layer experience. You can build dashboards and semantic models in Omni, Looker, or a similar BI tool, and you design metric definitions that people and AI tools can both query correctly.

Git-based workflows. You keep pull requests small and well described, and you give thoughtful code review.

Nice to have: Redshift performance tuning (sort and distribution keys), Python for scripting or API pulls, reverse ETL, and experience with product event, billing, payments, or ecommerce data.

Around 7 or more years in analytics engineering or data engineering where you personally shipped production data models.

Several years of hands-on work building and maintaining production dbt projects.

Working directly with sales teams and RevOps, and owning reporting they relied on to run the business.

Owning a data domain over time and evolving its models as the business and its questions changed.

#7212. Product Manager - Postgres at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're hiring a Product Manager to own Postgres itself at Supabase. Every Supabase project is a full Postgres database, and Postgres is the foundation that Auth, Storage, Realtime and Edge Functions run on. You'll own the product experience of the database: Postgres versions and upgrades, maintenance, observability, extensions and security.

You'll work closely with our Postgres engineering teams and with the PM lead for Databases, who leads Multigres. You'll define the Postgres requirements that architecture must meet, and own how customers experience their database through that transition.

This role is ideal for someone who loves Postgres, sweats the details that make a database trustworthy, and is excited about improving the experience for millions of developers. Small improvements here compound across every project on the platform.

You'll own Postgres versions, upgrades and end-of-life. Which major and minor versions we support, how and when customers upgrade, and how we retire old versions. Your goal is to make upgrades routine rather than risky.

You'll make maintenance predictable. You'll design how we schedule, communicate and run maintenance windows so customers stay informed and in control, and disruption is rare and expected.

You'll help developers understand their database. Query performance, metrics, logs and advisors: you'll own the path from "my database is slow" to the fix.

You'll own our extension ecosystem. Which extensions we offer, how we evaluate and support new ones, and how we keep them working across versions.

You'll own Postgres security. Roles and permissions, Row Level Security ergonomics, secure defaults, and how we respond to Postgres and extension CVEs.

You'll talk to developers across the full spectrum, from indie developers on their first project to enterprises running critical workloads, and bring what you learn into every prioritization call.

You'll own the problem definition, requirements and success metrics for what we ship, such as upgrade success rate, the share of the fleet on supported versions, and time-to-diagnose for performance problems. You'll set the metric before the team builds, then track it after launch.

You'll keep Engineering, Support and GTM aligned. You'll own the plan of record for what's coming, why, and in what order, and communicate version changes and deprecations clearly to customers.

4+ years of Product Management experience on databases, developer tools or infrastructure, or equivalent depth as an engineer, DBA or founder moving into product. You're comfortable commenting on an architecture doc and advocating for the user when trade-offs are required.

Know Postgres well, or another relational database well enough to ramp quickly. You're comfortable reading EXPLAIN output and talking about replication, WAL, roles and extensions.

Have shipped products where reliability and trust are the experience. You treat downtime and breaking changes as product problems, not just operational ones.

Write clearly. Your specs, announcements and deprecation notices leave little room for confusion.

Build with what you own. You run queries, test upgrades and try extensions yourself, and form opinions through first-hand use.

Use AI to move faster. You use AI tools to compress research, drafting and feedback synthesis so you spend more time on judgment calls.

Work async by default. Supabase is fully remote. You make decisions in writing and move work forward without meetings.

Ran Postgres in production: upgrades, tuning, incident response.

Worked on database observability, performance tooling, or security and compliance products.

Contributed to Postgres, its extensions, or the wider open-source community.

#7209. Texas Democrats propose bills to nix state DEI ban at public colleges

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The party clinched the state’s House and governorship in the midterm elections, though that still may not be enough to get the legislation passed.

In the state’s House, Reps. Nicole Collier and John Bucy III have put forward bills that would repeal the 2023 law. Texas state Sen. José Menéndez introduced a companion bill in the state’s Senate.

At any other time in the past three decades, the new bills might have faced little chance of passage simply by virtue of being championed by Democrats. But the party now controls two-thirds of a trifecta for the first time since 1996 — a makeup that may still not be enough to pass the legislation.

Senate Bill 17 — the target of the new bills — bans several DEI activities at public colleges, including:

Texas was one of the first states to ban DEI offices and initiatives in higher education. Following SB 17 and a similar law in Florida, universities from Ohio to Iowa to Alaska shuttered their DEI offices, often under legislative mandates.

Institutions quickly responded to SB 17. For instance, the University of North Texas said before the law took effect that it would restructure its Multicultural Center and Pride Alliance into the student affairs office and launch a new Center for Belonging and Engagement.

After passage, the Texas A&M University System issued guidance stating that DEI terminology couldn’t be used on the institution’s websites. Offices dedicated to student affinity communities would have to close, though it said some programs and libraries could be absorbed into broader offices and initiatives.

Texas Tech University also closed its DEI office.

Texas Tech University System’s latest chancellor — Brandon Creighton,  who helped author SB 17 — has gone even further than the bill’s requirements, which don’t cover academic content. The system has moved to eliminate academic programs focused on sexual orientation and gender identity and put restrictions on teaching about race, sex and gender in courses.

State audits so far have determined that the University of Texas System, nearly all of the institutions within theTexas A&M University System and 19 of the state’s community colleges are compliant with SB 17.

Audits last year deemed two institutions noncompliant — a community college because it made a new employee participate in a DEI training and Texas A&M-Central Texas because it struck a contract that didn’t conform to the law’s ban.

Curious about the kind of challenges you'll work on at Camunda? Watch this quick 30-minute talk from our engineers to learn more about the new Camunda Exporter and how we’re solving complex problems at scale

We are looking for a Product Builder to join the IDP & RPA pod. You will take real problems from customers, decide what is worth solving, and build and ship the solution yourself, using AI tools to get from idea to production quickly without lowering the bar.

Product Building is how Camunda's Experience organization builds the applications people use on top of Camunda 8, such as Hub, Operate, and ProcessOS. Small pods of builders own a product area from customer problem to shipped change, with no hand-offs between product management, design, and engineering. We are setting this model up now, so you will help shape how it works.

The IDP & RPA pod builds how Camunda processes work with documents and legacy applications. With intelligent document processing (IDP), customers create extraction and classification templates in Camunda Hub and publish them so a process can use AI to turn invoices and other documents into structured data. With robotic process automation (RPA), customers integrate legacy applications that have no APIs, using Robot Framework scripts on a standalone RPA worker. The hard problems come with putting LLMs into customer processes: extraction quality, model and prompt choices, testing and trust, and working for AI agents as well as for people.

Help shape product direction for IDP & RPA by combining customer input, usage data, and field feedback into clear priorities.

Partner with the other builders and product strategy to turn problems into shipped features, using short written proposals and prototypes to align early.

Make judgment calls with incomplete information within your scope and own the outcome.

Communicate trade-offs and decisions clearly to stakeholders who don't live in the details the way you do.

Use AI tools across the whole loop, from specs to prototypes to production-ready code, and hold a high bar for what you ship by reviewing and improving the output rather than accepting it.

Build, test, and ship improvements to the IDP & RPA experience, reading errors and tracing issues far enough to give your pod useful context.

Talk to customers and to support and customer success colleagues about how IDP is used and where it breaks, and turn what you learn into decisions about what to build next.

Pick up customer incidents and trace them across Hub, the connectors, and the deployed process of the customer.

Track what you ship against real outcomes and adjust when the data tells you to.

Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Hi there! We're hiring an Information Security Engineer with a focus on Application Security to join our Information Security team at Camunda.

We are a team that takes security seriously without making it feel like a locked door. We work in a transparent, straightforward way, and we care about doing the work right rather than just checking a compliance box.

You will work side by side with our R&D engineers: reviewing designs, threat modeling new features, digging into code and helping teams make good, risk-based decisions about the vulnerabilities that come in. You'll also help build the internal tools that make this work scale, including our CVE registry and the LLM-assisted workflows we use to analyse code and triage findings.

Camunda is an AI-first company, and InfoSec is no exception: AI agents are part of how we review code, assess vulnerabilities, and build our tools. You will help shape how that works.

This is a hands-on role for someone who already has solid AppSec foundations and wants to grow. We are a generalist team, so you will also take part in our on-call rotation and incident response, and get exposure to other areas of security. But most of your time will be spent with engineers and building and maintaining internal tools and processes.

Vulnerability management: triage findings from SCA, container and code scanning, external reports and internal security testing; assess whether Camunda is actually affected; agree fix / mitigate / accept with engineering and follow through to release.

Threat modeling and security reviews: run threat modeling sessions and design reviews with R&D teams for new features and architecture changes, and turn the results into concrete, prioritised actions.

Code analysis with LLMs: use and improve LLM-assisted tooling (agents, prompts, reusable skills) to review code, validate findings and reproduce issues, while applying your own judgement to know when the model is wrong.

Build internal security tooling: contribute to the CVE registry and related automation that connects scanners, engineering teams, and customer communication. Much of it is built on Camunda itself.

Help engineers do the secure thing by default: answer questions, review pull requests, improve guidelines and occasionally run a session or workshop.

On-call and incident response: share the InfoSec on-call rotation with the other engineers and help investigate and resolve security incidents.

Support the wider team: help write security advisories for our customers, answer customer security questions and support audits.

Around 3+ years in security or software engineering, with at least 2 of them working on application or product security (AppSec, product security, pentesting, or a security-focused developer role all count).

Experience at a SaaS or software product company, working closely with engineering teams that ship often through modern CI/CD and cloud environments.

Hands-on vulnerability triage: you've worked with tools like Snyk, Trivy, Semgrep, CodeQL or similar, can tell a real issue from a false positive, and can explain how exploitable something actually is in context (CVSS, exploitability, configuration).

Threat modeling and design review experience (e.g. STRIDE) on real systems, ideally web apps, APIs, or distributed services.

You read and write code. You're comfortable reading Java (Camunda is built on it) well enough to follow a data flow and spot a security bug. You can write scripts and small tools and contribute to an existing codebase with review.

You build with AI, not just chat with it. You use AI coding agents (Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot or similar) in your daily work to write tools, analyse code or automate tasks. And you know their output still needs a second look, especially when it comes to security.

Clear, friendly communication with engineers. You see yourself as an enabler, not a gatekeeper, and you can explain risk to people who aren't security specialists.

Some Python and/or JavaScript experience, or experience building internal tools.

Willingness to learn and use Camunda, since we build a lot of our own security automation on it. Prior BPMN / workflow experience is a plus.

Experience responding to security incidents or being part of an on-call rotation.

Exposure to security advisories, CVE disclosure or bug bounty programmes.

Knowledge of AI/LLM security risks (OWASP Top 10 for LLMs / agentic apps, prompt injection, tool abuse).

Experience securing Kubernetes or container-based workloads, or open-source / B2B software.

#7184. Attorney, New Verticals at Atticus

At any given time, 16 million Americans are experiencing a crisis that requires urgent help from our legal system or government. The right assistance could transform their lives. But today, most never get it.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured person in crisis to get the life-changing aid they deserve. In the last six years, we've become the leading platform connecting people with disabilities to government benefits. We’re also quickly scaling an injury practice to help victims of accidents, misconduct, and violence get compensation from insurance.

So far, we've helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10B in life-changing aid — and earned over 20,000+ five-star reviews. And we're just getting started.

We've raised more than $100 million from top VC firms like Forerunner, GV (Google Ventures), and True Ventures, with ambitions to create a category defining business assisting Americans in need.

Each month, tens of thousands of Americans seek our help for a single legal matter – applying for Social Security Disability, filing a Workers’ Compensation claim – but many of these clients are actually eligible for additional aid without ever knowing it.

Your job as Lead Attorney, New Verticals is to help us do right by these clients, helping them claim additional benefits through the social safety net. You’ll oversee the team of intake specialists who speak with clients who have secondary claims, evaluating the case facts and whether to connect them with an Atticus attorney or local attorney to provide legal advice and potentially take on their case.

This is a job that straddles the line between practicing the law and growing a business. It requires developing a strong understanding of multiple fields of law, and being able to accurately identify when we can help and when we cannot. But even more importantly, this job requires you to be an incredibly strong operator: you need to keep the big picture in mind and stay laser-focused on how we can achieve our goals, oftentimes finding outside-the-box ways to accomplish them. You will report to our General Manager of New Verticals and work closely with our CEO and Managing Attorney.

On any given day, you’ll spend your time:

Speaking with severely injured clients trying to rebuild their lives after a catastrophic accident

Sourcing and vetting local counsel in individual markets, holding a high bar for representation quality and ending relationships that fall below it

Working with our data and insights team to identify existing clients who may have unrecognized claims in adjacent fields of law, and setting the standard for which ancillary cases we take on

Building the playbook for claim types we haven't handled before, starting by screening cases yourself and eventually creating the protocol our intake specialists will run at scale

This is a rare opportunity for an attorney to join a profitable and growing public-interest law firm and take on one of our biggest priorities as a company.

You can understand the nuances of various contingency fields of law and can apply them to a variety of cases (including Social Security Disability, Workers’ Compensation, Personal Injury, and more) – you don’t need to be an expert today, but you should be a fast learner.

You can empathize with anyone and quickly build rapport and relationships while exercising good judgment, both with our clients and local counsel at law firms across the country.

You have strong instincts and judgment as an operator – you can run effective and efficient processes, and you don’t let them run you.

You have a demonstrated commitment to doing good and helping people.

You have meaningful work experience outside of serving as a lawyer, ideally at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment (e.g., a business operations team).

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues

We anticipate the base salary band for this role will be between $140,000 and $175,000 in addition to equity and benefits. The salary at offer will be determined by a number of factors such as candidate's experience, knowledge, skills and abilities, as well as internal equity among our team.

This job is fully remote and we’re committed to empowering everyone with flexibility. Work remotely, and travel to LA (on the company dime) as needed to be with your colleagues – usually quarterly, plus offsites. #7182. Education Department faces management challenges from recent changes, watchdog says

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

An annual federal report cited interagency agreements and a handful of executive orders as key factors that will challenge the agency in 2027.

Since early 2025, the second Trump administration has outsourced many of the Education Department’s core functions such as grant management, monitoring and technical assistance to other agencies through interagency agreements. The department has also reorganized many of its offices and laid off a bulk of its staff.

"Change of this magnitude can result in challenges associated with employee resistance, fatigue, and uncertainty — such as decreased productivity, operational disruption, and lost efficiency — and have an impact on the organization’s ability to respond to stakeholder questions and concerns," the Nov. 13 report from OIG stated.

The Education Department has not yet issued an annual performance plan for fiscal year 2026 and has yet to issue its annual performance report for FY 2025, strategic plan for FY 2026-2030, or annual performance plan for FY 2027, according to the report.

Other reports through various federal offices have flagged similar concerns with the Education Department's output amid McMahon's efforts to close the agency, which ultimately requires Congressional approval.

In June, another OIG report found that layoffs at the Education Department appear to have impacted units that were performing legal duties.

The staffing changes were made to 16 of the department's 17 offices, resulting in an overall reduction of at least 40% of the more than 4,000 employees who were working for the Education Department when Trump reentered office in January 2025.

Many lawmakers, civil rights groups and lawyers have raised concerns with output from at least one of those offices, the Office for Civil Rights, which saw a slowdown in case resolutions that included no sexual harassment or assault cases being settled in 2025. A later report from the office of Sen. Bernie Sanders, I-Vt., showed that OCR resolved only 1% of its cases overall in 2025, the lowest percentage from any OCR in over the last decade.

Responding to a draft of the most recent OIG report, the Education Department said it had made progress in many of the areas of concern, including change management. According to OIG, the department said it has "continued efforts to improve operational processes and maintain program delivery during a period of substantial organizational change." Additionally, the agency said it has "engaged in contractor support to assist with resolution for external audits," among other adjustments.

The department has previously maintained that its staffing and organizational changes will not affect the statutory functions it is legally obliged to perform.

The department also stressed in a comment to K-12 Dive that the OIG report is an annual update. In a January OIG report, the office flagged similar oversight challenges stemming from executive orders and resulting organizational changes.

"It is critical that the Department strategically plan for and effectively manage these and other major operational changes that it may decide to undertake," it warned at the time.

Past reports have flagged challenges under previous administrations, such as monitoring grantees' use of COVID-19 pandemic relief funding.

#7179. Engineering Manager - Pipelines at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Pipelines team at Supabase. Supabase Pipelines is a managed change data capture product. It copies the tables you select from Supabase Postgres to other systems, then streams later changes. The product is in public alpha, with BigQuery, ClickHouse, DuckLake and Snowflake destinations.

Pipelines uses Postgres logical replication and Supabase ETL, our open source replication engine written in Rust. The team works close to the database. That means replication slots, WAL retention, initial table syncs, and the load they place on a customer's source database. The team also builds and runs the destination connectors. Correctness, ordering and recovery matter there as much as speed.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

Build a healthy, high-trust team culture in a remote-first, globally distributed environment.

Run 1:1s, performance management, career development, and onboarding for your direct reports.

Handle people admin (leave, time off, etc.) for your team.

Own the hiring plan for your team and act as Hiring Manager. First round of interviewer for all roles on the team.

Partner with Talent Acquisition to source, interview, and close strong candidates.

Contribute to headcount planning, org design, and the evolution of engineering leadership at Supabase.

Own the team's roadmap and project management (Linear), driving accountability and clear milestones.

Manage workflow intake and Slack triage. These are the team’s primary inboxes for cross-team requests. You decide what gets routed where, prioritized, or pushed back on.

Own the team's weekly updates and keep stakeholders aligned without creating overhead.

Organize effort against support tickets and maintain the team's SLO.

Surface risks, dependencies, and tradeoffs early and clearly to leadership.

Train as an Incident Commander. Drive coordination, resolution, and post-incident follow-up.

Have 5+ years of engineering background, with enough depth to be credible with engineers building complex systems.

Have 3+ years managing remote engineering teams.

Have managed a team that built and ran data pipelines, change data capture, replication or streaming systems.

Have strong technical credibility in data systems. We look for delivery guarantees, ordering, idempotency, schema changes and backfills.

Know Postgres replication well, including logical replication, the WAL and replication slots, and how they affect a production database.

Know analytical destinations such as BigQuery, Snowflake, ClickHouse or lakehouse table formats like Iceberg.

Have led a team through production incidents on a system that holds customer data. You ran the follow-up work and kept the review blameless.

Have shipped a developer-facing data product to customers outside your company.

Have experience with Rust (not required but a plus)

Have managed remote, globally distributed teams that work asynchronously across disparate time zones.

Are technical enough to be credible with strong engineers, but genuinely passionate about people management. This is the work you want to do, not a track you're settling for.

Build psychological safety and a feedback culture. You give feedback often and in the moment, ask for it back, and create space for the team to do the same.

Operate as a multiplier. You help individuals achieve more than they could alone, and you don't need to be the best technical voice in the room.

Coach rather than direct. You remove blockers, connect people to context, and trust your engineers to own how the work gets done.

Have a real track record of performance management and can speak to specific examples of difficult conversations.

Are comfortable with ambiguity and can build structure where there isn't much.

Communicate clearly and write well. You default to async and written, set context early, and surface risks early.

Are collaborative, low-ego and adaptable. You thrive with high-autonomy senior engineers who don't need you to be the smartest person in the room.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

#7176. Engineering Manager - Realtime at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Realtime team at Supabase. Realtime lets developers send low-latency messages between clients, track and synchronize user state, and listen to database changes. Those three features are Broadcast, Presence and Postgres Changes. Broadcast also sends from the database and over HTTP, and clients can replay recent messages.

Realtime is a globally distributed Elixir cluster built on Phoenix. The team balances low latency, fair limits between projects, and the cost of running the service. Customers expect connections to stay up, so reliability matters as much as new features.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code, but you you will lead a team of experienced engineers. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

This pivotal role will shape our publishing portfolio, requiring the industry relationships, market knowledge and commercial judgement to recognze exceptional games and distinguish genuine potential from attractive pitches.

Beyond finding games, the successful candidate will assess their commercial potential, development teams' ability to deliver, and positioning within the competitive landscape.

They will own the scouting pipeline, from identifying opportunities and building developer relationships through to evaluation, internal presentation and negotiation. They will also help establish the processes and standards that will underpin our scouting operation as the business grows.

Focused on impact. You understand the 80/20 rule and focus on activities that matter.

Performance-driven and growth-oriented. You have an intrinsic will to succeed while unafraid to try, fail, and learn by experimenting with new approaches and testing ideas.

Cooperative. You help others succeed; you give and receive feedback well.

Humble. You ask for help; you are quick to admit and learn from your mistakes.

SMART. You work and communicate in ways that are Specific, Measurable, Actionable, Relevant, and Time-bound.

Identify and source exceptional PC and console games through industry networks, events, direct relationships and digital channels. Focus initially on Steam and Epic Games Store, identifying distinctive games with genuine commercial potential.

Consider potential for streaming, content creation and community engagement, alongside development team capabilities, budgets, schedules, commercial potential and key risks.

Drive evaluation and signing by reviewing pitches, prototypes and playable builds, producing structured assessments and presenting recommendations internally. Coordinate due diligence, lead or support negotiations with prospective publishing partners, and maintain developer relationships throughout the process.

Build the scouting function by establishing processes, evaluation criteria and pipeline management. Monitor market trends and emerging opportunities, report on potential signings, and help recruit and develop the scouting team as the business grows.

Significant experience in PC/console publishing, scouting, business development or a related commercial role, with an established independent developer and studio network and a track record of identifying and progressing promising games.

Excellent knowledge of PC and console markets, genres, competitors and player expectations, alongside practical Steam experience, including publishing, launching games and building audiences.

Strong creative and commercial judgement, with the ability to evaluate gameplay, market potential, development capability and financial viability.

The confidence to make independent recommendations, challenge assumptions and communicate a compelling commercial rationale, supported by excellent relationship-building, presentation and cross-functional collaboration skills.

Compensation. We pay based on qualifications, experience, and fit for the role. We provide an allowance to purchase your own device or equipment and offer variable compensation through our annual bonus program.

Growth. We offer an environment where you will have the trust and freedom to try, fail, learn fast, and make a real impact while accelerating your career, and where anyone with a great idea can pitch it directly to our management team.

You’ll work closely with Finance, Engineering, Legal, and our vendor partners.

This is a lead role for the Procurement function. You'll own the technical and analytical side of vendor management, reporting, efficiency, and the commercial interface across the company, while keeping procurement governance tight. Communicate clearly across both technical and non-technical audiences.

Have experience in async or globally distributed teams.

Are energized by building a function from the ground up and navigating ambiguity.

At any given time, 16 million Americans are experiencing a crisis that requires urgent help from our legal system or government. The right assistance could transform their lives. But today, most never get it.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured American to get life-changing aid. Our mission is to tear down barriers between people in crisis and the aid they deserve. So far, we’ve helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10 billion in life-changing aid —and earned over 20,000 five-star reviews. And we’re just getting started.

Each day, thousands of people seek our help getting disability benefits or insurance compensation. They’re intimidated by the process, unsure where to turn, and desperate for good advice.

As a Legal Assistant at Atticus, your job is to ensure they get and stay on the right path. This job is different from that of a traditional legal assistant. The job mixes responsibilities in any good legal assistant’s comfort zone (build client relationships; support paralegals and attorneys in filing and managing claims) with those unique to being an operator in a fast-growing tech company (help Marketing source clients who’d be great in testimonials, or user test new tools for Product).

In a typical week you might do any of the following:

Make contact with a client that’s in danger of slipping through the cracks at SSA, and help get their case back on track.

Work with a client’s team to run down missing paperwork at SSA.

Take on an inefficient workflow that’s slowing the team down (e.g., processing case updates), scope an improvement, get team buy-in, and execute the improvement.

This role is a rare opportunity to join a fast-growing Series C startup and do meaningful social impact work. It’s designed to be a path to building expertise in the fields of law you support and taking on greater responsibilities over time (e.g. becoming a paralegal).

You have a Bachelor’s degree from a four-year university with strong academic performance.

You have experience in a professional role that required a substantial amount of customer-facing work and/or administrative tasks.

You are a resourceful team-player ready to dive into a scrappy startup environment where you will learn quickly and get your hands dirty.

You are comfortable in a variety of interpersonal conversations; you can be empathetic and kind when speaking with people in a moment of crisis, and can be thoughtful and organized when interacting with attorneys or the SSA.

You have a demonstrated commitment to doing good and helping people.

You have professional experience helping others apply for government benefits.

You have experience at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment.

We are strongly committed to building a diverse team. If you’re from a background that’s underrepresented in tech, we’d love to meet you.

This is a rare opportunity to join a startup that has strong traction (substantial funding, well-respected backers, tremendous growth, and many happy customers) but is still small enough that you can have a huge impact and play a role in shaping our culture.

We’re a certified B Corporation tackling a critical social problem. Our mission to help people in need drives everything we do, and your work here will touch many lives.

We offer competitive pay — including equity for every employee — and generous benefits:

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

15 vacation days & ~20 paid holidays each year (including two weeks at the end-of-year)

$1,000/year reimbursable stipend for education and training outside of work

Up to $1,200/year student loan repayment assistance

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues

We anticipate the base salary band for this role will be between $50,000 and $75,000 in addition to equity and benefits. The salary at offer will be determined by a number of factors such as candidate’s experience, knowledge, skills and abilities, as well as internal equity among our team.

This job is fully remote and we’re committed to empowering everyone with flexibility. Work remotely, and travel to LA (on the company dime) as needed to be with your colleagues – usually quarterly, plus offsites. We care a lot about building a great culture and we think some interactions need to happen in person, so we put a lot of thought into retreats, offsites, and other ways to gather.

#7161. Public Policy Lead - US at Kindred

Kindred is a members-only home swapping platform that unlocks the ability to live a travel-rich lifestyle through the power of community. By exchanging primary residences with trusted peers, renters and owners alike can match with Kindred spirits and explore new destinations without breaking the bank.

Kindred is revolutionizing the “third option” of travel accommodation, compared to short-term rentals and hotels. Since launching in 2022, Kindred has grown to a global community of 300,000 members across 150+ cities and puts home swapping on the map as a radically more affordable, responsible, and connected way to travel.

To support that momentum, Kindred has raised over $150M in capital from some of the most credible investors in Silicon Valley, including Index Ventures, Andreessen Horowitz, New Enterprise Associates, Bessemer Venture Partners, Caffeinated Capital, Elad Gil, and founders of companies like Opendoor, Figma, ClassPass, and more.

Kindred’s co-founders are proven leaders from the early team at Opendoor ($15B+ exit), who have each built and scaled products that today generate $1B+ annual revenue combined.

We’re looking for the world’s top builders, executors, and believers to help us make travel more meaningful.

You can learn more about us in Forbes and The New York Times.

This is Kindred’s first dedicated public policy hire. You’ll join a three-person legal and policy team (reporting to the General Counsel) and own the execution of Kindred’s public policy and government relations efforts, with a focus on North American markets but covering additional markets as necessary.

The role is primarily focused on proactive advocacy to shape the policy environment for home-swapping. You’ll build relationships with elected officials, regulators, and industry groups. You’ll commission and manage economic impact research. You’ll develop city-level partnerships that normalize home-swapping as a category distinct from other travel categories. You’ll also develop working familiarity with the tax, housing, and accommodation regulations that intersect with Kindred’s model, and help parse how those frameworks apply (or don’t) to credit-based home-swapping. You’ll do all of this initially as a team of one, with outside consultants to support you and a path to building a team as the company grows and the function matures.

Execute Kindred’s public policy strategy with an emphasis on proactive advocacy.

Build and maintain relationships with elected officials, regulators, and staff at the local, state/provincial, and national levels. Represent Kindred in meetings, hearings, coalition convenings, and industry events.

Track proposed housing, short-term rental, tax, and platform regulations that could impact Kindred’s model, identifying where Kindred’s credit-based, non-commercial structure creates meaningful regulatory distinctions. Use AI-assisted tools and other technology to monitor developments across jurisdictions.

Further develop and evolve Kindred’s core policy narrative and positioning materials. Draft position papers, comment letters, testimony, and advocacy materials.

Propose and negotiate city-level partnerships that promote and normalize home-swapping. Build relationships with housing advocacy organizations, tourism boards, trade associations, and aligned industry coalitions.

Commission and manage third-party economic analyses on the impact of Kindred’s model, collaborating with internal business teams and outside consulting firms.

Identify, evaluate, and manage outside consultants cost-effectively (lobbyists, policy advisors, research firms).

Partner with internal teams to develop externally facing materials and data packages for stakeholder engagements.

Coordinate with the General Counsel on lobbying compliance, political activity disclosures, and regulatory filings.

7–10 years of experience in public policy, with at least 3 years at a technology company or similar fast paced environment.

Experience building or meaningfully contributing to building a public policy or government relations function, not just operating within an established one.

Track record of productive relationships with elected officials, regulators, and policy stakeholders.

Experience managing outside consultants, lobbyists, or policy advisors across multiple jurisdictions.

Strong written and verbal communication skills, including experience drafting policy position papers, advocacy materials, or regulatory comments.

Experience with policy matters at the city and state level in the United States, with an interest in policy work in new jurisdictions.

Fluency with AI tools and a willingness to integrate them into policy monitoring, research, and content development workflows.

Willingness to operate as an individual contributor while building the function, with the judgment to know when to engage outside support.

Experience in or adjacent to the sharing economy, home-sharing, travel, housing, or short-term rental sectors.

Experience at a growth-stage company (under 500 employees).

Familiarity with vacation/short-term rental, housing, and land use regulations, as well as related tax regulations.

Comfort with detailed regulatory analysis: ability to read and interpret statutes, ordinances, and administrative rules, and translate their implications into actionable policy positions. A law degree and legal practice experience is a plus but not required.

Build something new: You’ll help define the function and shape how Kindred engages with governments and regulators from the ground up.

A genuinely different model: Kindred’s credit-based, non-commercial home-swapping model is structurally distinct from existing travel lodging categories, contributing to sustainable tourism objectives.

Path to leadership: As the company and function grow, this role offers the opportunity to greater leadership.

Competitive compensation: Base plus meaningful equity in a growth-stage company.

At Kindred, we know that good things happen when we look out for one another. We offer a thoughtful, human-first benefits package designed to support health, wellbeing, and connection.

Our openings may span more than one career level. The base salary offered depends on factors such as experience, transferable skills, business needs, impact, and market data.

Flexible time off, plus 12 paid holidays per year

21 credits per year to stay in Kindred homes

$4,000 annual travel stipend to help offset travel-related costs

Remote-first and remote-flexible work environment, with opportunities to work from anywhere and gather in person, including at Kindred HQ in San Francisco

Regular team and company offsites to connect, collaborate, and build together

A vibrant, inclusive, and highly skilled team that ferociously protects team chemistry

A culture of high standards, feedback, and continual growth

We are committed to providing equal employment opportunities for all applicants and employees. Kindred does not discriminate on the basis of any protected characteristic, including race, color, ancestry, national origin, religion, creed, age, disability, sex, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, medical condition, genetic information, family care or medical leave status, marital status, domestic partner status, military and veteran status, or any other characteristic protected by US federal, state or local laws, or the laws of the country or jurisdiction where you work. Pursuant to the San Francisco Fair Chance Ordinance, we will consider for employment qualified applicants with arrest and conviction records.

Share a written point of view with the entire growth team on who our established creator buyer is and the three biggest gaps in how we sell to them. And an action plan on how we can win these creators.

You'll run a quarterly win/loss program and share what you learn openly, build a clear path for sales leads on our website, and make sure launches that matter to larger accounts ship with both a self-serve and a sales-assisted version. Your work has a direct impact on helping Kit reach $100,000,000 ARR while protecting the self-serve engine that got us here.

#7155. 6th Circuit upholds jury win for Ohio State in former graduate student’s sexual harassment lawsuit

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

While the plaintiff argued the district court incorrectly instructed the jury, the 6th Circuit said she’d failed to raise these objections at the proper time.

A former Ohio State University graduate student could not convince a federal appeals court to overturn a jury’s verdict in favor of the school in her sexual harassment lawsuit because she failed to show that her trial was unfairly prejudiced, according to a Tuesday decision.

The plaintiff in Huang v. The Ohio State University alleged that a faculty advisor subjected her to unwanted touching and that, when she resisted his advances, he retaliated by manipulating her candidacy exam for an engineering doctorate. She brought claims under Title VII of the 1964 Civil Rights Act as well as the 14th Amendment’s due process right to bodily integrity.

A district granted summary judgment for Ohio State on the plaintiff’s Title VII claims, holding that she was not an employee of the university within the law’s definition and therefore could not bring those claims. At trial, a jury held for the plaintiff’s advisor. She appealed to the 6th U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals, which reversed and ordered a new trial.

At the second trial, the jury determined that the plaintiff was not an employee because Ohio State and the advisor did not have the “right to control the manner and means by which [the plaintiff] accomplished her work.” The jury also ruled in favor of the advisor on the plaintiff’s 14th Amendment claim.

The plaintiff again appealed to the 6th Circuit, alleging that the district court incorrectly instructed the jury on what facts were relevant to its determination of whether she was an employee. She also claimed that the court abused its discretion in excluding certain evidence and that it improperly prohibited her from requesting economic damages in her closing argument.

Each of these claims failed, the 6th Circuit held.

The plaintiff forfeited her challenge to the court’s jury instructions because she failed to raise it, despite the district court specifically asking whether the parties had objections or edits to its instructions. To grant a third trial on this point “would be unfair to the court and the defendants,” the 6th Circuit said.

In her evidentiary argument, the plaintiff claimed that the district court improperly prevented her from submitting portions of a slideshow containing the results of an Ohio State campus climate survey on sexual assault. She argued that the presentation would have made her allegations more plausible.

However, the plaintiff failed to include the relevant slides in her appeal, leaving the 6th Circuit unable to conclude whether they had sufficient weight to affect the jury’s verdict. The appeals court also could not determine whether the slides offered anything “above and beyond” other testimony in the case.

In all, the plaintiff failed to show that any of her allegations amounted to unfair prejudice warranting a new trial.

The case represents one in a handful of recent jury decisions to favor employers facing Title VII lawsuits. In March, a jury held for Haribo of America in a former employee’s discrimination and retaliation case, determining that the plaintiff’s dismissal was a mutually agreed-upon separation between the parties rather than retaliation.

And in a 2025 case, a jury sided with Levi Strauss in a lawsuit alleging that a former executive did not receive a promotion because of her pregnancy, gender and age.

#7147. Head of Product - AdTech at Yodo1
