From games like Transformers: Earth Wars, Rodeo Stampede and Ski Safari, to bringing iconic IPs from Hasbro, AMC, Netflix, Paramount and Sony Pictures to leading studios like Rivergame, Candivore, 37Games and Tencent Studios, we connect creativity with global expertise.

Our work spans game development, publishing, IP licensing and ad monetization across mobile, PC and console. We build games, grow franchises and help developers reach players worldwide. We love games. We play games. And together, we grow games that last.

We believe in the power of games and that everyone deserves the opportunity to build a life they love, from anywhere in the world.

We're fully remote by design. We embrace Work From Anywhere, with no fixed hours or limits on time off. We trust our people to own their work and deliver results.

We're driven by performance, curiosity, and continuous improvement. We experiment often, challenge assumptions and value impact over titles.

We are looking for an entrepreneurial Head of Product, AdTech to help shape the future of mobile game growth.

We're looking for someone who sees beyond today's products. Someone who understands the entire advertising ecosystem, can identify opportunities others overlook, and has the vision and technical judgment to turn ambitious ideas into successful products.

You'll be a strategic partner to our leadership, helping define where we go next, what we build, and how we win.

Entrepreneurial. You think like a founder, see opportunities where others see problems, and love building products from zero to one.

Visionary but practical. You can see where an industry is heading while knowing exactly what needs to be built today.

Customer-obsessed. You understand customers' businesses, not just their feature requests, and solve problems that create measurable value.

Technically curious. You understand how advertising systems work and are excited about what AI and automation make possible.

A builder and a coach. You bring people together, raise the bar for product thinking, and help teams turn ideas into exceptional products.

Performance-driven and growth-oriented. You have an intrinsic will to succeed while unafraid to try, fail, and learn by experimenting with new approaches and testing ideas.

SMART. You work and communicate in ways that are Specific, Measurable, Actionable, Relevant, and Time-bound.

Own the long-term product vision, strategy, and roadmap across our AdTech business

Shape how AI and automation transform mobile monetization and user acquisition, making sophisticated advertising capabilities accessible to developers of every size.

Connect monetization, user acquisition, and data into an intelligent growth ecosystem that helps studios make better decisions and grow profitably.

Take products from concept to launch and scale, making clear prioritization decisions and holding yourself accountable for measurable customer and commercial results.

Extensive hands-on experience building and scaling products in AdTech, mobile advertising, or adjacent performance marketing technology.

A strong understanding of the mobile advertising ecosystem, including ad monetization, mediation, user acquisition, DSPs, SSPs, bidding, attribution, and advertising economics.

A proven track record of taking complex technical products from idea to launch and delivering measurable commercial impact

Experience working with data-intensive platforms, optimization systems, machine learning, or AI-powered products.

Strong commercial judgment and the ability to connect product decisions to customer outcomes, revenue, and business growth.

This role blends TPM expectations with the Delivery Product Owner operating model: the Sr TPM sets product direction, priorities, success metrics, and major tradeoffs; the TPM helps ensure that direction becomes shippable, well-understood, and well-supported product increments.

#7142. Alabama lawmaker eyes 3-year bachelor’s degrees for public colleges

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The forthcoming proposal would allow the state’s universities and community colleges to create reduced-credit programs in high-demand fields.

Orr framed his proposal as a way to help affordability and meet workforce demands.

“Alabama higher education institutions need to be more innovative and accommodating to meet the needs of students in the 21st century,” he said on social media Saturday.

The Commission on Colleges and Universities, the accreditor that oversees most public colleges in Alabama, has already paved the way for three-year degrees in the state. In March, it approved a formal pathway to allow for the creation of reduced-hour bachelor’s degrees with 90 to 100 required credits.

Traditional bachelor’s degrees typically require at least 120 credits.

“We want institutions to have the flexibility to design programs that help students reach their goals efficiently while ensuring that the degrees they earn continue to reflect the quality and credibility that accreditation represents,” Stephen Pruitt, the accreditor's president, said at the time.

Though much of the discussion around accelerated bachelor’s degrees is theoretical, the model has gained increasing popularity in the last few years.

In early 2023, no U.S. colleges offered three-year reduced-credit bachelor's degrees , according to the nonprofit Jobs for the Future. Since then, some 100 such programs have been announced or launched.

Some states have already approved such programs.

A 2025 Iowa law directed the state’s three public universities to create three-year bachelor's degrees as a means of addressing workforce demands and decreasing the cost of college attendance. The state board of regents signed off on two such program proposals this month, pending regulatory approval.

One is an online bachelor’s in applied professional studies at the University of North Iowa, expected to launch next fall.

Deirdre Heistad, the university’s interim associate provost for academic affairs, said in a Nov. 12. statement that the new program will serve adults with some college credit but no credentials, many of whom “work as educational support professionals, service coordinators or in other fields where a bachelor’s degree is increasingly expected at the next level.

At least two private institutions in Iowa, Coe College and Upper Iowa University, already offer an accelerated bachelor’s program.

And in September, the Oklahoma State Regents for Higher Education changed its policies to allow the creation of four new accelerated bachelor’s degrees at three public universities.

Other states are still weighing the concept.

This summer, the State Council of Higher Education for Virginia announced it would partner with Ohio higher ed officials to create a blueprint for 90-credit bachelor’s degrees. Both states have laws mandating that bachelor’s degrees from their public colleges require at least 120 credits.

#7128. Head of Research at CoinMarketCap

CoinMarketCap is hiring a Head of Research to turn CMC data into research that institutions, media and partners trust and pay for. This is a senior, hands-on role reporting to the CMO.

CMC is the most referenced price and market data source in crypto. Our research already reaches millions each month through reports like our Monthly Exchange Report, Asset Watch and Market Pulse, and is cited by Bloomberg, WSJ, Forbes and Fortune. The next step is different. We are building CMC into a data platform for crypto, tokenized assets and broader markets, and research is how we prove the quality of that data to the people who buy it.

You will own our research agenda end to end: what we publish, how rigorous it is, and how it supports our enterprise data business. You will also be the senior voice on sensitive market analysis for CMC and its partners.

Set and run the research roadmap, from recurring flagships (monthly exchange report, weekly market and asset coverage) to deep dives on exchanges, tokenized assets, RWAs and market structure.

Build institutional grade research that showcases CMC data to banks, funds, data buyers and enterprise API clients, working closely with our API and BD teams on what buyers need.

Own methodology and data integrity: define how we measure volume, liquidity, flows and reserves, document it, and make sure every published number holds up to scrutiny.

Lead research on sensitive or fast moving market events, balancing speed, neutrality and accuracy, and handle review requests from partners with sound judgement.

Be a credible public face for CMC research with tier one media, on panels and in interviews.

Manage and develop a small research team, currently one research analyst, and use AI and automation to scale output without lowering quality.

Partner with marketing, editorial and product to turn research into formats people actually read and share, from long form reports to charts and short social posts.

7+ years in research or data analysis, with at least 3 in crypto or digital asset markets.

A track record of published, data heavy research that institutions or serious media rely on.

Deep understanding of market structure: exchanges, spot and derivatives, liquidity, onchain flows and proof of reserves.

Strong quantitative skills and hands on data work (SQL or Python, APIs, large datasets).

Excellent written English and the judgement to present sensitive findings neutrally and accurately.

Experience leading or mentoring analysts.

Background at a crypto data provider, an institutional research desk or a TradFi data business.

Understanding of how data is packaged and sold to institutions (licensing, enterprise feeds, research subscriptions).

Knowledge of tokenized assets, RWAs or traditional market data.

Existing relationships with tier one financial and crypto media.

Comfort using AI tools to automate data pulls and drafts.

Mandarin, given our close work with Chinese speaking partners and media.

#7125. AI Native Head Of Growth - EdTech (French fluent 🇫🇷) at Paradox

Most growth teams call themselves data-driven. They have a dashboard, an agency and a ChatGPT licence to write ad copy faster. That’s the 99%.

We want to be part of the 0.1%: a company where AI doesn’t just help the growth team, it runs the growth loop. Every day, on every channel.

Then it’s your chance to define a new category : The Operating System (OS) for Human Greatness Augmented with AI.

Science-backed. Design-led. AI-powered. Global.

We’re not looking for someone who only advises on what to test, we’re looking for someone who builds it. A hands-on Head of Growth who can ideate, write the copy, design the strategy and launch experiments directly.

1. Generate hypotheses. Read the signals (ad performance, webinar attendance, sales calls, objections, churn reasons, comments, competitors’ ads) and propose what to test next, with a cost and an expected impact.

2. Act across every tool. Not a chatbot in a tab: it reads and writes in our whole stack, from ad accounts to CRM, landing pages, webinars, email, social DMs and analytics.

3. Hold the full context of Paradox. Our DNA, offers, voice, member stories, past campaigns and what they taught us, in one shared company brain.

4. Ship its hypotheses. Produce the hook, the ad cut, the page, the email sequence or the funnel variant, and put it live.

5. Measure with real KPIs. Every test is tied to a number that matters (cost per qualified lead, show-up rate, close rate, CAC, payback, LTV), not to likes or reach.

6. Learn and loop on its own. Scale what works, kill what doesn’t, write the lesson back into the brain, and launch the next cycle without waiting for a meeting.

This is already real. Our growth hub tracks every hypothesis and experiment, with its result. An ads library of thousands of creatives feeds our creative research. Agents cut ads from long-form content, prepare webinars, wire Instagram comment-to-DM automations and audit our tracking after every live. Athena, our AI coach, works with members every day.

We believe self-help is broken: generic theory, a quick high, no real change.

We believe people deserve more. More than content. More than motivation.

They deserve a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

Growth at Paradox means bringing people into something that actually changes their life, and proving it.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals)

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

Paradox was founded by David Laroche, a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson and Gad Elmaleh. We started in French. We’re building for the world.

Here’s the bet: a company that learns by itself grows in a way a company of people alone can’t. A small growth team running a self-learning machine can out-test, out-learn and out-grow teams a hundred times its size.

We hold ourselves to the standards of Apple, Spotify and MasterClass. While others hide their numbers, we let our results speak.

As Head of Growth, you’ll be the architect of a growth machine that learns by itself. In a classic growth team, people do the work and AI helps. Here it’s the other way round: agents do the work, you design the system, set the bar and make the calls.

You don’t just manage a team, you get your hands dirty to identify growth levers and pull them yourself.

❌ The old way: a Head of Growth hires people and agencies, briefs them, and checks their work.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: your team is mostly agents, and you build them yourself. You write their skills, plug them into our tools (ad accounts, CRM, webinars, email, analytics) and write the evals that tell an agent what "good" means: a hook that stops the scroll, a page that converts, a test that’s worth its cost.

You give autonomy step by step: first an agent suggests, then it acts once you approve, then it acts alone within a budget and guardrails. You move each agent up a level only when its track record earns it.

Example: in your first month you build the agent that reviews every ad set overnight. At first it only suggests. By month three it pauses losers and moves budget by itself within limits you set, and you only see what was decided and why.

❌ The old way: a media buyer checks the dashboards each morning, changes bids by hand and waits for the weekly report.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: you build and improve the agents that watch every ad set, page, webinar and email sequence around the clock. They spot what’s dropping, find the cause, fix it or launch a variant, and report what they did.

You give autonomy step by step: an agent can pause an ad on its own long before it can raise a budget. You decide where each agent stops and a human takes over.

Example: show-up rate drops on Tuesday’s webinar. Before anyone notices, an agent has found the cause (a reminder that never went out), fixed it and started testing a new reminder sequence.

❌ The old way: a creative team briefs an agency, waits two weeks and gets five ads.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: you build the agents that turn every live, interview and member story into hooks, ad cuts and shorts, that study what the best advertisers in the world are running, and that write in our voice.

Your taste becomes the eval: you write down what makes a great hook, a great cut and a great story, in rules and examples the agents check themselves against. Each time you reject something, the reason goes back into the rules.

Example: one webinar becomes dozens of hook variants by the next morning. You keep the best few, and next week the agents’ first drafts are already closer to your bar.

❌ The old way: an analyst rebuilds the numbers in a spreadsheet at the end of the month, and nobody trusts them.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: you build the agents that connect every touch to cash, from ad to lead, live, sale and transformed member, and that audit the tracking after every campaign.

No number, no test: you define the KPIs that matter (CAC, show-up, close rate, LTV, payback), and agents refuse to launch a test that isn’t wired to one.

Example: after each live, an agent cross-checks the ad platform, the CRM and our analytics, finds where they disagree and fixes the leak before the next live.

❌ The old way: lessons live in people’s heads and leave with them.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: every result goes back into the company brain, and agents read it before proposing anything. The machine never learns the same lesson twice.

You measure the machine itself: tests per week, time from idea to live, the share of the work done without a human, and how many agents moved up an autonomy level this month. You push those numbers up.

And always: no fake scarcity, no fake claims, real stories told with consent. The machine can scale, but it doesn’t decide what’s right. You do.

How you’ll know you’re AI-native: you spend your time building agents, writing evals and making calls, not doing the work by hand. Most experiments are generated and shipped by agents. The output grows every month while the headcount doesn’t.

You’ll own these KPIs: LTV / CAC ratio, funnel conversion rate by stage, experiments launched per week.

Desired outcomes: conversion rates 2x better than baseline, growth experimentation that runs continuously, a predictable growth engine.

Tools you’ll master: GA4, Optibase, Vidalytics, Clarity, Hotjar, Amplitude, Figma, Looker Studio, VWO, Optimizely, Claude Code, and whatever helps you see what others miss.

You have ideally a minimum of 3 years of experience in Conversion Rate Optimization, Growth Experimentation, or Performance Marketing, ideally within SaaS, e-commerce, or digital product environments.

You have a track record of designing, implementing, and scaling A/B and multivariate tests that have generated measurable improvements in revenue, user activation, or conversion.

You’ve built with AI and agents yourself: Claude Code, automations, APIs, evals, creative pipelines. Show us something you built, not a certificate.

A Conversion Architect, skilled at diagnosing funnel bottlenecks and translating data into structured experimentation roadmaps that deliver measurable business outcomes.

A Behavioral Strategist, fascinated by what drives human action, able to mix cognitive psychology, UX, and persuasion frameworks to design experiences that convert because they resonate.

A Data-driven Creator, fluent in GA4, Hotjar, AB Tasty, VWO, Amplitude, not just running tests, but connecting insights to revenue, retention, and lifetime value.

Loop thinker: you see every channel as a loop that can learn, and you can’t stop yourself from closing it.

Taste: you know a great hook in two seconds, and you’d rather ship one great ad than ten average ones.

Numbers-honest: you say when a test failed. You care about real revenue, not vanity metrics.

Builder, not manager: you’d rather build the system than coordinate an agency.

Growth over ego: you love hard feedback, you read, study and learn fast.

Cross-disciplinary: you move from psychology to spreadsheet to creative brief to code.

Analytical: you turn a vague problem into a clear hypothesis, a metric and an experiment.

English: Fluent English, you can hold a conversation on specific details with English-speaking colleagues.

Our chosen language for written communication is English.

❌ If you need clarity before chaos — wrong place.

❌ If you prefer balance over intensity — wrong season.

❌ If you build to deliver, not to elevate — wrong mission.

❌ If you want to optimize without getting your hands dirty in landing pages, copy, or design — wrong role.

❌ If you want to manage agencies and budgets rather than build a machine — wrong role.

✅ If you’re obsessed with psychology, systems and creative…

✅ If you want to build the growth machine that learns faster than anyone else…

✅ If you dream of using AI to bring transformation to millions of people…

Build one of the first truly self-learning growth machines, and write the playbook others will copy.

Grow something that genuinely changes lives, with the proof to show it.

Work with filmmakers, psychologists, engineers, and creatives obsessed with impact.

You’ll compete with San Francisco companies.

-> 🏅 Being surrounded by A-players who strive to perform

But you don’t just want a competitive paycheck, you also want…

-> Playing a crucial role in the development of a company whose content and products will be used by millions of people

-> Waking up in the morning feeling like you are making a difference is something!

We receive messages every day from clients who have successfully lost weight, quit smoking, reached a milestone in their business, built better relationships, and made positive changes in their lives.

But that’s not all, we believe that driven people deserve…

-> 😎 A company culture based on autonomy & accountability

-> 🗽 Flexible working hours (but very hard working)

-> 🌎 A remote culture from day one with the freedom to work from wherever you want

-> 🧍‍♀️ Standing desks paid by the company if you live far from our offices

-> 🏀 350€ per year paid by the company to take care of yourself (for your gym, or dance class or whatever make you feel great)

-> 📚 150€ of books or education - At Paradox, we are learner lovers and we want to support you in that

-> 🔥 Access to Athena - our Paradox AI Coach supporting you on anything personal or professional, we want you to live your best life

⚠️ To complete the recruitment process, each candidate must organize calls with his or her previous managers. If you are not comfortable with this : do not apply ⚠️

If all this sounds good to you, come and write the next chapter with us 🚀🚀🚀🚀

📃 A 6 to 8 minutes application survey (with a short video assignment)

💻 A 30mn video call with Timy, HR, to deep dive into your career path and ambitions

💻 A 1h video call, to discuss how you’d fit into Paradox’s Growth team

💻 A 1h culture-fit video call, to assess alignment with our values and mindset

💻 A 1h final video call with David, CEO, to discuss the growth vision and the next milestones to build

💼 Reference check to understand how you operate across environments and help set you up for a smooth onboarding

Our recruitment process is tailored to do our best to avoid that you or us realize that we’re not a perfect fit. Every step serves a purpose and will allow us to get to know you better as much as it’ll help you understand more where you’ll be stepping in.

We also want you to have time to ask all of your questions regarding the mission, the long-term vision of your team and the company, our values, with the relevant interlocutor in front of you.

If you’re not joining to tweak landing pages.

If you’re here to engineer behavioral change at scale, for millions.

Let’s build the Operating System for Greatness.

#7123. Graduate Software Engineer, Open Source and Linux, Canonical Ubuntu at Canonical

We are hiring 2026 and 2027 Graduate Software Engineers into engineering teams around the world. As a global platform company we offer a wide range of software engineering challenges, in both open source products and commercial business systems. We have teams that work in a variety of languages, typically Python, Golang, Rust, C/C++, JavaScript and of course... Bash. We work from the very lowest levels of the Linux system - BIOS and boot loaders, firmware, kernel and drivers, all the way up to desktop applications and containers. We work on tiny devices like the Raspberry Pi and new RISC-V boards, all the way up to supercomputers, clouds and Kubernetes clusters. We work on compilers and toolchains, and we work on security, cryptography, performance and documentation. Some of our teams focus on packaging thousands of pieces of software, others focus on deep contributions to a single body of code.

If you are excited about the potential that open source has for humanity and the enterprise, you have outstanding results in your academic career, and you are excited to contribute to the open source stack, this is the best way to apply to Canonical. We will work with you to identify teams and projects that would be interesting and a good fit for your skills and motivations. The process is highly competitive, it will require effort and excellence to succeed if you apply here.

Most of our engineering teams work from home. We try to align a single team in a single time zone - EMEA (Europe, Middle East and Africa), APAC (Asia Pacific and Indian Ocean), and AMER (North, South and Central Americas). Even though you work from home, we try to ensure you always have colleagues alongside to coach and mentor you, and for the companionship of shared problem solving and pair programming. Larger projects have multiple teams and might span the globe. We work with the open source community and specialize in building teams that work well in the 'fishbowl' of open source engineering. This is full time work, five days a week, often solving hard problems with significant responsibility. We don't generally work weekends and nights and we do enjoy good holidays but we also expect productive and intense engagement Monday to Friday.

We bring all our engineering teams together in person twice a year for a deep 'sprint' with other teams to stimulate discussion and enable a wide range of career development paths. This is a fantastic way to demonstrate and develop your skill in software, learn about a wide range of technologies, make a meaningful impact to open source, and see some very interesting new cities that you might never otherwise get to visit.

If you find the open source stack amazing, and you have been a consistent high-performing student in your school and university, then this will be a challenging and exciting start to your career.

Location: This is a Globally remote role.

We consider geographical location, experience, and performance in shaping compensation worldwide. We assess performance and revisit compensation twice annually to ensure we recognize outstanding colleagues. In addition to base pay, we offer a performance-driven annual bonus or commission. We provide all team members with additional benefits which reflect our values and ideals. We balance our programs to meet local needs and ensure fairness globally.

Canonical is a pioneering tech firm at the forefront of the global move to open source. Our platform, Ubuntu, is very widely used in breakthrough enterprise initiatives such as public cloud, data science, AI, engineering innovation, and IoT. Our customers include the world's leading public cloud and silicon providers, and industry leaders in many sectors. We are founder-led, profitable, and growing.

Powered by worldwide distributed collaboration since our inception in 2004, we believe tech and business leadership can emerge anywhere. We have no central headquarters. Most of us work from home, but we are exceptionally disciplined about this. We have very high expectations of all colleagues, and are champions of excellence. Working here is a step into the future and will challenge you to think differently, work smarter, learn new skills, and raise your game.

We are proud to foster a workplace free from discrimination. Diversity of experience, perspectives, and background create a better work environment and better products. Whatever your identity, we will give your application fair consideration.

#7117. AI Native Creative Strategist - EdTech (French fluent 🇫🇷) at Paradox

What if your next playground was to build a global, iconic Brand, with a creative machine that never sleeps, and compounds?

We’re not looking for a "Social Media Manager" to follow a calendar. We’re looking for an AI-native Creative Strategist (Brand & Content).

Someone who understands that to win today, you don’t just need "content", you need a Movement.

This isn’t another content job. Most content teams use AI to write captions faster. That’s the 99%. We want to be in the 0.1%: a media where AI runs the creative loop, from the signal to the hook, the cut, the post and the lesson. Every day, on every platform, in French and in English. You bring the taste. The machine you build brings the scale.

Here’s the bet: a company that learns by itself grows in a way a company of people alone can’t. We aim for $10M in revenue with 10 people, each of them running a dozen agents.

Paradoxically, the more AI runs our content, the more human it has to feel. AI doesn’t make more of what everyone makes. It makes more of what only we can make.

1. Read the signals. Comments, DMs, retention graphs, saves, shares, search trends, objections from sales calls, and what the best creators and advertisers in the world are publishing right now.

2. Hold the full context of Paradox. Our DNA, years of interviews, lives and courses, member stories, and every past post with what it taught us, in one shared company brain.

4. Ship. Format, schedule, publish and answer, on every platform.

6. Learn and loop on its own. Double down on what works, kill what doesn’t, write the lesson back into the brain, and start the next cycle without waiting for a meeting.

At Paradox, we are convinced that greatness is trainable, and we believe self-help is broken.

People deserve more: a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

That’s why we want to empower people at scale, by leveraging cutting edge technologies like AI, and crafting user experiences with outstanding aesthetics.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals)

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

Paradox was founded by David Laroche, a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson and Gad Elmaleh. We started in French. We’re building for the world.

You lead the Content Factory not by management, but with creativity.

In a classic content team, people do the work and AI helps. Here it’s the other way round: agents do the volume, you design the machine, set the bar and make the calls. Humans do what no machine can: taste.

Build a symbiotic strategy where David Laroche’s personal brand and Paradox’s company brand reinforce each other without cannibalization.

Implement radical new formats (documentaries, conceptual shorts) that shatter the boring codes of the "Self-Help" industry.

Define distinct, sharp editorial lines for each platform: Depth & Search for YouTube, Aesthetic & Vibe for Instagram, and Authority & Authenticity for LinkedIn.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents watch every platform, every competitor and every comment continuously. They tell you which angle is rising, which format is tiring and which story our audience asks for. The strategy updates every week, on evidence.

Take responsibility for the entire production chain from hooks to storyboarding the narrative, and ensuring the final cut matches the vision.

Own the packaging: titles, thumbnails and the first 3 seconds. You test variants every week and know your click-through and retention numbers by heart.

❌ The old way: one shoot becomes one video and a handful of posts.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: one shoot, one live or one interview becomes dozens of hooks, shorts, carousels and ad cuts by the next morning, in both languages. Concepts are tested with AI-generated drafts before we spend a day shooting them.

Your taste becomes the eval. You write down what makes a great hook, a great cut, a great carousel and a great story, in rules and examples the agents check themselves against. Each time you reject something, the reason goes back into the rules.

Act as the final quality check: nothing gets published if it doesn’t meet the "Paradox Standard" of aesthetics and storytelling, whether a person or an agent made it.

❌ The old way: a creative team briefs an editor, waits three days and gets two versions.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents draft, cut, caption, translate and schedule. You keep the best, and next week their first drafts are already closer to your bar.

Analyze retention graphs, CTR, and CPL to iterate based on feedback, killing what fails and aggressively scaling what works.

❌ The old way: someone pulls the stats at the end of the month and nobody changes anything.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents read the numbers of every post every day, explain why it worked or didn’t, and propose the next test. You measure the machine itself: posts per week, time from idea to live, hit rate, and the share of the work done without a human.

We started in French. You build the machine that takes Paradox to English-speaking audiences: channels, formats and voice that feel native, not translated.

And always: real stories told with consent, no fake claims, no fake scarcity. The machine can scale, but it doesn’t decide what’s right. You do.

How you’ll know you’re AI-native: you spend your time on concepts, taste and calls, not on resizing, captioning and exporting. The output grows every month while the team doesn’t.

Proven Content Builder: You have successfully grown a media brand, a YouTube channel, or a personal brand to significant scale (100k+ followers or millions of views). You understand the mechanics of virality and retention. You’ve created at least one format that broke out, and you can show its click-through and retention numbers.

Elite Scripting Skills: You can show us scripts you wrote that performed. You know how to structure a narrative, write a hook that stops the scroll, and keep an audience engaged for 20+ minutes.

Performance Fluency: You have worked in environments where content had to drive business results (ads, launches, conversion), not just vanity metrics.

AI-native Creative: you’ve built content pipelines with AI yourself: Claude Code, automations, generative video, image and voice tools, evals. Show us something you built, not a certificate.

Intolerant to Mediocrity: You have "Taste." You are physically pained by a bad font, a clumsy cut, or a weak headline. You fight for the details that others ignore, including in what AI produces.

Psychology Obsessed: You don’t just "make content"; you study human nature. You constantly ask why people click, why they share, and what emotion drives them.

#7116. Federal student lending caps are ‘blunt instrument’ to prevent defaults, researchers say

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

A “richer set of criteria,” including program earnings and credit profiles, would better address that risk, according to a new paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of Philadelphia.

The Republican spending bill enacted in 2025 ended the Grad PLUS lending program and set hard lifetime caps on federal student lending at $100,000 for graduate students and $200,000 for professional students — a classification being disputed in court.

Republicans aimed to reduce student debt loads through the law. When it passed, Rep. Tim Walberg, the Republican chair of the House Education and Workforce Committee, said the loan overhaul would help ensure “students don’t borrow excessive debt they can never repay.”

To examine the potential impact of the caps, the Philadelphia Fed research looked at three-year default rates among borrowers who took out federal graduate student loans and entered repayment between 2011 and 2016.

The overall default rate for those borrowing over the new limits — in inflation-adjusted dollars pegged to 2025 — of 6.5% for graduate students compares to a 3.3% rate for those borrowing below the limits. Master’s students drove up that rate, as they defaulted 7.2% of the time when borrowing above loan cap levels.

But the size of students’ federal loans wasn’t the only factor that predicted defaults.

Another was borrowers’ credit risk profiles. Three-year default rates for all graduate borrowers declined as their credit scores improved, the Philadelphia Fed found.

Borrowers with credit scores between 300 and 579 — deemed “deep subprime” — defaulted at a 12.4% rate. That rate declined until it reached just half a percent for those with scores between 770 to 850, the highest credit scores.

In every credit score bracket, students who borrowed above the new limits defaulted more often than those who borrowed less than the capped levels.

But those credit profiles don’t tell the full story, the researchers noted. The earnings potential of students’ fields of study also played a major role in defaults. Sub-prime and near-prime borrowers who studied in the highest-earning programs defaulted less frequently than less-risky prime borrowers in lower-earning programs.

“Loan limits are a far coarser signal of loan performance than either creditworthiness or program characteristics,” the researchers wrote.

But under the new caps, riskier student borrowers could be locked out of higher-earning programs by a private lending market that is expected to take over a larger share of student lending.

Following the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections, the federal loan caps could come under political pressure. Rep. Bobby Scott, the ranking Democratic member of the House education committee, has blasted the federal loan caps.

In March, for example, he and other Democrats on the committee said that the lending structure will “further restrict higher education access, particularly for low- and middle-income borrowers” and “destabilizes our higher education system and harms students.”

However, any changes to the new lending system in the near term would require sign-off by President Donald Trump or enough Republicans joining with Democrats to overturn a veto.

#7112. Senior Product Designer - Product Growth at Kit

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. For coaches, YouTubers, authors, podcasters, and other creatives, there isn't a better marketing hub to rely on to grow audiences, automate email marketing, and sell digital products — all within one platform.More importantly, there isn't a team more committed to helping creators earn a living. We're on a mission to help creators earn $1 billion using our creator marketing platform. We have always been 100% independent and 100% remote.We are proud to have built a product that our customers love, and we look for people who have enthusiasm and belief in our mission, vision, and values to join our team. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

We’re looking for a senior product designer to be the first dedicated designer on our product growth team, shaping how creators get set up, see value in their first days, and enjoy a seamless upgrade experience. Kit ships fast, and the product growth team builds the frameworks and systems that connect creators to the value in their plan as the product keeps changing. We need a designer who can balance rapid experimentation with long-term value for our creators.

Product growth is a new team at Kit, formed earlier this year. In the six months since, we’ve found our rhythm and started to move the numbers, but we’re still shaping how we work, how we prioritise and where we focus in the long term. As the team’s designer, you’ll be the design voice for growth, with real influence over how the team works and what it builds.

You'll own the design of the moments that move our funnel: onboarding, activation, free trials, upgrade and upsell flows, pricing and checkout, and billing. You'll work in a team that learns by shipping, through A/B tests and ship-and-measure changes, and you'll use data to decide what to try next.

This role looks beyond the team. You’ll keep a pulse on what other product teams are shipping and how marketing communicates, so that what creators are promised before sign up matches what they find during onboarding. We’re looking for someone who enjoys looking across Kit to see what's changing, then focusing on what it means for their own work.

This is a full-time IC role for a designer who loves connecting design decisions to numbers, moves quickly without sacrificing quality, and is excited about using AI to accelerate outcomes.

We need someone who thinks deeply about user behaviour, interaction design and the product experience, and who is proactive about finding the next opportunity rather than waiting for a spec.

You'll report to Dan Winer, Director of Product Design, and join a small, high-impact product design team currently consisting of four senior product designers and a senior design engineer. From day one, you’ll be part of the product growth team’s product trio alongside the product manager and engineering manager, shaping the team’s vision, priorities and how we work. You’ll also work closely with the team’s engineers.

You’ll work closely with product marketing on how we talk to creators before and after they sign up, and with the other product designers to stay ahead of what’s shipping across Kit. You’ll also partner with data and our creator success teams.

First week: Get to know Kit’s product and the product growth team’s goals. Get access to Figma, Linear, Mixpanel and our other reporting tools. Take part in get-to-know-you calls with the team.

First month: Get up to speed on how we track and measure creator behaviour in Mixpanel, Omni and Clarity, which experiments are running now, and what we’ve learnt from past ones. Join the product trio’s planning and prioritisation discussions, and ship a small experiment or two, with support from the Director of Product Design as you take ownership of the growth work.

First six months: Own design across the team’s work, which spans growth (onboarding, activation, trials and upgrades) and billing (checkout, plan changes, downgrades and cancellation). This might include personalising onboarding, improving free-to-paid conversion or simplifying our payment flows. By six months, the team should rely on you for a clear picture of how creators move through these flows, where they get stuck and why. You’ll help the team run more and better experiments, from forming a hypothesis to reading the results. We don’t have dedicated researchers, so you’ll talk to creators, run usability tests, track the impact of your work and proactively suggest iterations.

First year: Help the team improve our activation, free-to-paid, trial conversion, and early retention rates, so that more creators upgrade, stay on Kit, and find real value. Turn our upsell, discount and plan change moments into one coherent experience instead of a set of one-off prompts.

Interaction design for complex web applications, with a strong eye for visual hierarchy and detail.

Grounding design decisions in the creator’s voice through interviews, usability testing and customer feedback, partnering with the PM so it shapes what we build as well as how.

Designing flows that change depending on who’s seeing them, like free, trial and paid accounts, or creators upgrading and downgrading.

Designing onboarding, activation, upgrade, pricing, checkout or billing flows.

Experiment design and reading results, including knowing when an A/B test is the right approach or when to just ship and track.

Defining what events to track so the team can measure what you shipped, using tools like Mixpanel.

Clear written and visual communication of design rationale, including async walkthroughs.

Around 5+ years as a product designer on SaaS or other complex web-based products.

Led design on growth, monetisation or onboarding work and can point to its impact on metrics, ideally including retention or quality of conversion, not just volume.

Worked in a cross-functional team with a product manager and engineers, shipping continuously.

Worked within and contributed to an established design system.

A portfolio that shows how research or data informed your decisions and what happened as a result.

Bonus: designed pricing, billing or plan change experiences.

Curious about why users behave the way they do, and about what the numbers aren’t telling you.

Comfortable being wrong in public and sharing what you don’t know, not just what you do.

Proactive and self-directed, with a bias for shipping.

Excited about AI and pragmatic about how to use it.

Low ego and comfortable giving and receiving direct feedback.

Preferring to execute designs handed down from a product manager.

Treating a conversion win as the finish line, without checking whether those creators stay with Kit and find value.

Wanting long, highly-refined projects with little contact with data or results.

Discomfort with experiments that fail or with changing direction based on what we learn.

Focusing on visual polish without deeper thinking about user behaviour and business outcomes.

Being philosophically opposed to using AI in your work.

Needing well-established processes and a fully defined roadmap from day one.

We're 100% remote with team members across the globe. We work mostly on East Coast time - so if you're based on the West Coast of the United States, Europe, or Asia, you might have a few early mornings or late evenings. We do our best to keep things reasonable, but a little time zone juggling comes with the territory.

Kit has standardized salaries based on position, no matter where you live. For this role, we're hiring at level 4 ($172,000). Level is determined based on experience and our interview process.

We cover up to $2,280 per month toward medical premiums, with dental and vision premiums fully covered. We offer Health Insurance plans through Aetna

$2,000 equipment allowance for your first two years, $1,000 budget every following two years. Company-provided laptops are issued to every Kit team member and are not included in the equipment budget

Individual learning + development budget ($3,500/year)

Creator meetup budget ($50/month) to connect with creators over coffee, lunch, or other in-person meetups

Childcare benefit up to $3,000 annually through JOON

Twenty (20) days of paid time off during each year of employment

Paid paid vacation: An after-tax bonus of $1,000 for taking five consecutive days of vacation where you’re fully unplugged from work

#7102. Court approves $5.4M bankruptcy sale of Bacone College assets

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

An environmentally focused real estate firm is buying the property and other assets of the Native American-serving institution that shuttered last year.

Along with Bacone’s campus, the sale includes two parcels of land that together total about 67 acres. It also includes a trove of books, archival materials, paintings and other artworks, many of them with significance to Bacone’s history and Native American communities.

ODP and its CEO, Reed Oppenheimer, did not immediately respond to questions Tuesday about the firm’s plans for the Bacone property and other assets.

The real estate firm has made efforts to get certain “significant buildings” of Bacone’s onto the National Historic Registry, according to court testimony recounted in the judge’s order.

The property also has a small cemetery. Oppenheimer must provide “reasonable access to those wishing to visit a grave site,” the judge wrote.

The order approving the sale came over the objection of a former Bacone professor who raised numerous concerns, including that the Muscogee Nation might have a claim to the land under a 19th century treaty. The judge wrote that testimony and documents provided to the court confirmed the legal transfer of land that ultimately came under Bacone’s ownership.

Bacone — originally named Indian University — began teaching classes in a Baptist mission house in 1880.

In the early 20th century, the institution deepened its focus on Native American education, recruiting Native staff and faculty and developing classes in Native American culture. In the 1930s, Bacone established an arts center that developed a distinct aesthetic and expressive movement.

By the 21st century, some faculty members questioned the college’s commitment to Native American students and faculty. In its last years, only a handful of faculty were Native American, and its Native student makeup fluctuated widely, dipping to as low as about one-third in 2018.

In fall 2023, Bacone had just 106 students, less than a tenth of the 1,184 students it enrolled in 2010. By February 2024, The Associated Press reported that the college was teetering on the edge of closure amid lawsuits, debts and the risk of accreditation loss. When visiting the campus, a reporter for the news service found buildings in disrepair, molding Native works of art and accusations that administrators had embezzled funds.

When Bacone initially filed for bankruptcy, leaders intended to keep the college open. But after a federal bankruptcy trustee found evidence of financial mismanagement, a judge ordered the college into Chapter 7 bankruptcy. That kicked off a court-managed liquidation of what was, until its closure, the oldest continuously operating college in Oklahoma.

Own the architecture of Raya's iOS codebase: set the patterns and best practices for state management, SwiftUI adoption, navigation, networking, and concurrency, and solve the hard client-side lifecycle problems that span teams.

Drive modernization of a large, long-lived Swift codebase: modularization, SwiftUI alongside UIKit, Swift concurrency, and deliberate management of tech debt. Write the tech specs, make the trade-offs explicit, and sequence the work so it ships incrementally.

Build the shared foundation other iOS engineers build on: design-system components, shared libraries, networking and persistence layers, and the dev harness (build times, CI, test infrastructure, release tooling).

Build and roll out the AI-collaborative development tooling iOS engineers use every day, and set the standards for how we use it on the client.

Improve client production health infrastructure: crash and performance monitoring, alerting, release quality, and the security posture of the app.

Partner with server engineering on API design and client-server state sync, and with the product client teams so new features land on solid patterns.

Raise the engineering bar through design reviews, code review, mentorship, and clear written specs.

5+ years of professional iOS development experience, with strong proficiency in Swift, UIKit, and MVVM.

Strong experience with RxSwift or similar reactive programming frameworks, and with Redux-style state management patterns.

A track record of shipping consumer mobile apps to large audiences, with strong judgment on architecture, performance, and user experience.

Experience owning client platform or infrastructure work that other engineers depend on (modularizing a large iOS codebase, build and CI systems, shared libraries, design systems, or release tooling) and driving its adoption.

Experience with mobile security: device trust, certificate pinning, secrets handling, or security remediation on the client.

#7084. Senior Implementation Engineer at Clutch

Clutch helps credit unions launch digital lending and account opening. As a Senior Implementation Engineer, you're the product specialist who solves the hardest technical problems on those projects.

You specialize in one Clutch product and cross-train on a second. You join each project at key moments and whenever it needs something beyond the default setup. An Engagement Manager owns each project and the relationship with the credit union.

This role fits someone who can diagnose a complicated setup, explain it to a non-technical audience and decide when a request is worth building.

Diagnose complicated setups and find working answers through configuration, mapping, integration design and workarounds

Design and build non-standard configuration, core mapping and integrations

Decide whether a requested change is worth making. When it isn't, say no, explain why and offer an alternative

Own the testing strategy and lead live testing sessions with credit union teams

Advise our implementation team on what's possible, what needs a workaround and what's a true product gap. File clear tickets with our product and engineering teams for the gaps

Help write and improve our best-practice default setup, workbooks and test scripts with our Product and Engineering teams

Coach Engagement Managers to run standard setup themselves

Use AI to speed up your work, and standardize what works with the team

Work on many projects at once. Occasional onsite visits with customers.

Our Professional Services team implements Clutch for our credit union customers. Every project starts from a best-practice default, and we change it when the change improves the credit union's outcome. We test the setup with the credit union and take them live. Our top metric is customer success through renewal and expansion. We look for people with high agency, sound judgment and a strong technical foundation, who keep teammates informed.

Our company is growing quickly, and so is our team. We're looking for people who want to help shape a world-class delivery motion.

Learn our default setups, decision log and the core and vendor systems around your product

Join your first projects alongside a tenured Implementation Engineer

Complete onboarding and lead the technical design on several projects

Handle your first non-standard configurations and integrations on your own

Be the go-to person for your product area

Contribute to our default setups, workbooks and test scripts

Coach Engagement Managers on standard setup

5+ years of experience in implementation, solutions engineering or technical consulting, ideally in fintech or banking software

2+ years integrating with web APIs. You read and write code (Python, JSON, JavaScript or similar), work comfortably in GitHub and the terminal, and debug payloads yourself

A record of diagnosing complex setups and finding working answers without engineering work

Sound judgment on which changes are worth making, and the confidence to say no and explain why

A grasp of why a product works the way it does and what it does for a customer's business

A named result from using AI in your work, such as time saved or a faster diagnosis

Comfort with ambiguity. You build a plan, tracker or checklist when none exists

The ability to explain technical concepts clearly to non-technical audiences

You communicate proactively. You flag risks early and keep Engagement Managers and customers informed

The ability to keep many projects moving in the same week

Experience with core banking or credit union core systems, Alloy, Stripe, Plaid or ACH

Unforgettable Off-Sites: Twice a year, bond with colleagues in exciting destinations, fostering teamwork and fresh ideas.

Clutch is a revolutionary vertical SaaS company, proudly backed by Andreessen Horowitz (A16z), aimed at revolutionizing the way Credit Unions engage and change the lives of their members. As a champion of financial well-being, we address the urgent need for affordable lending solutions in an era where the average American grapples with over $155,000 in household debt. Unlike traditional financial institutions, Clutch develops software to turn Credit Unions into FinTech lenders and leverage their balance sheets to responsibly lend to over 130M Americans. Our mission extends beyond mere financial transactions; we strive to fundamentally enhance the way credit unions interact with their members. By integrating cutting-edge technologies and user-centric designs, we help credit unions provide seamless digital experiences that are on par with leading tech companies. This approach not only preserves but revitalizes the longstanding tradition of community and member-focused service inherent to credit unions.
